| US0272 | independent-record-reviewer; agent; subagent (narrow brief: does the retrospective story describe SHIPPED behaviour) | sdlc-studio; agent; v1 | 2026-07-20 | Adversarial pass over the RECORD, not new code. Ran all three Verify commands and re-derived each claim independently. AC1's test asserts findings are non-empty AND all warn, so it cannot pass by silently dropping the placeholder. AC2 checked across the full type/status matrix (story Ready/In Progress/Done and epic, bug, cr all error; only story Proposed/Draft warn; missing status falls through to error) - the relaxation does not leak. AC3 re-derived in a clean repo: a Ready placeholder-only story exits 1 with missing decomposed, specified, verifiable. Chain checked: EP0088 Done, derived point total 2 matching US0272, US0272 the only child, CR0342 Complete via Decomposed-into, index rows agree with bodies. OVERCLAIM PROBE: transition to Ready SUCCEEDS on a pure-placeholder story; the story does not claim otherwise, but two shipped comments in validate.py did and were corrected. Findings: Affects omitted conformance.py though AC3's test targets it (fixed); User Story block still placeholders at Done (fixed); severity spelling warn vs warning makes three printed warnings report as warnings=0 (reproduced, filed BG0217). Verdict APPROVE. |
| US0276 | sdlc-studio; agent; v1 (author self-check, declared) | sdlc-studio; agent; v1 | 2026-07-20 | One-line rsync exclude. Both ACs executable and green: the exclude is present, and the pre-existing .local and bytecode excludes are asserted still present so widening the list cannot quietly narrow it. Verified by dry-run: the test cache no longer appears in the transfer list. NOT an independent adversarial pass - recorded as an author self-check so the record is not overstated. |
| US0276 | independent-reviewer; agent; subagent (narrow brief: one-line rsync exclude) | sdlc-studio; agent; v1 | 2026-07-20 | REJECT then repaired. MAJOR: AC1's verifier was VACUOUS - the verify DSL grep verb takes no -- separator, so 'grep -- --exclude=...' parsed -- as the PATTERN, matching nearly every line, and silently swallowed a nonexistent path. Proven by the reviewer: with the fix deleted from forward-port.sh the story still reported pass=2. Rewritten without the separator and with the dot escaped; it now reports fail=1 when the fix is removed. MAJOR: CR0370's own AC3 (an already-installed cache must not be stranded) was contradicted by the chosen mechanism and was missing from the story entirely - excluding a path removes it from rsync --delete's view, so the exclude converted a reaped orphan into a permanent one. --delete-excluded is NOT the fix, since it cannot tell junk from the consuming copy's .local state. Added an explicit reap scoped away from .local, added AC3 to the story, and verified end to end: a planted orphan is reaped and .local survives. MINOR: test residue in the source tree (scripts/tests/sdlc-studio) removed. NIT: CR0370's checkboxes ticked. Reviewer confirmed by mutation that both pre-existing excludes remain enforced. |

| US0477 | independent adversarial reviewer (fresh context, general-purpose) | Claude Opus 5 | 2026-07-30 | REJECT: - MAJOR: the epic story census compared the Epic field whole, so the 34 story files using the shipped template's linked form counted for nothing; EP0008's row was written as 7 against a true count of 18 and three rows were held as uncorroborated that simply had stories the reader could not see. FIXED as BG0426. - MAJOR: apply decided per row and wrote per epic id, so a multi-view index let one row's fillable verdict overwrite another row's held cell; apply printed "left alone" and wrote the cell anyway. FIXED as BG0427. - MAJOR: the header was matched by one literal spelling, making the sweep a silent no-op on a padded, unspaced or indented header, and lending one table's offsets to another table's rows. FIXED as BG0427. - MAJOR: an unparseable Dependencies row read as "declares none", so an epic minted from the full template declared no dependencies from an unrendered placeholder. FIXED as BG0427. - MINOR: the stat-signature memo can serve a stale census on a same-length edit with restored mtime, and on a whole-second-granularity filesystem. NOT FIXED - carried as a known issue. - MINOR: \_refresh\_epic\_row rewrites the whole index once per minted story (measured 11x on a 5-story batch) and emits unrelated hold warnings on every mint. NOT FIXED - carried. - MINOR: the refresh swallow is justified in not failing the mint and not justified in being silent. NOT FIXED - carried. - MINOR: the recorded REASON for the held rows was wrong - not deleted files but pre-tracking estimates. FIXED as BG0426. - MINOR: the non-epic control test does not cover the batch mint path. NOT FIXED - carried.; BLOCKING: none remaining. The four MAJORs are repaired and committed; the residue is filed and carried as known issues. |

NOT FIXED - carried. - MINOR: US0568's own normalisation step was dropped silently and its stated premise counted the story's own sentence rather than the corpus. NOT FIXED - carried. CONFIRMED: the unknown-lens sentinel holds against every case variant and padding, and is refused at the filing CLI too; the three detector-owed states are honest with exit codes matching the docs; register is strict where read\_ledger is lenient; every pre-mint attribution refusal fires; the backfill did not poison the cost estimator.; BLOCKING: BG0436 is the closest to a stop-ship - a shipped command tracebacks rather than reporting - but it needs an owed lens plus --file to fire, so it is carried as a known issue with that caveat stated. |

NOT FIXED - filed BG0436. - MAJOR: filing\_run resolves a two-id line by document order, so the refusal its criterion promises is reachable only when no run marker appears; a reversed carry-over phrasing promotes the carry-over to filing run. | US0620 | engineering-seat-boundary-r1 | claude-opus-5-author | 2026-08-03 | REJECT: [new] a genuine fix whose evidence mentions an artefact id and the word filed was misclassified as a deferral, and a genuine deferral worded without that word was misclassified as a fix, which then skipped the resolvable-id check entirely; [new] the repair row stores verdict\_date and reads it nowhere, so a round-one repair kept satisfying a later different rejection; [new] no help entry and no script-catalogue entry for the new repair verb, against the rule that a new command carries one; BLOCKING: [new] the closure-to-finding match is bidirectional substring, so a one-character closure closes every finding - repair --closed with a single letter marked a REJECT COMPLETE through the shipped CLI, flipped coverage to repaired and cleared the verdict half of the conformance gate, which is a review bypass; [new] the same substring rule in reverse silently closed a longer finding when a shorter one containing it was closed, so the residue went unnamed; [new] MUTANT SURVIVED - replacing both substring tests with exact equality left the whole suite green, so no test pinned the matching rule at all; [new] US0624 is FALSE through the command it names - the shipped preflight builds its coverage line through review\_coverage, which the diff never touched, so it still reported a repaired unit as covered by no independent review, and the criterion's own verifier called a private resolver instead; [new] US0624 declared Affects naming sprint.py while the whole implementation landed in sprint\_report.py, outside the declared scope; [regression] a pre-gate-grandfathered APPROVE now reads as rejected, because coverage\_state and critiqued\_unmet answer one question through two authorities, over 75 such rows in this repo's ledger; [new] coverage\_state never consulted sprint\_review\_for, so a unit covered only by an independent batch review was classed unreviewed AND named as the one real gap, manufacturing the false alarm the epic exists to remove |

| US0468 | Sam Eriksson (qa) + Dani Okafor (engineering), two independent fresh-context seats briefed with critic.py brief | Claude Opus 5 | 2026-08-05 | REJECT at the RUN-01KZ79C1 batch boundary, base fcdfe206. Both seats independently probed these four units and produced identical unit-level splits. US0468 AC2: the batch\_changes key set is a hardcoded alternation, not derived - adding and renaming a key both SURVIVED. US0480 AC3: the fixture carries a non-terminal status so it emits zero instances of the kind it claims to repair; AC5: neutering the ratchet command to return 0 left both lane tests passing. US0481 AC4: sprint.affects\_check is inert for sprint plan - a plan under warn and under block produces byte-identical output - and batch add writes the unit before it refuses it, the same shape BG0507 closed in this batch. US0637 AC1: \_COLLECTABLE short-circuits, so resolver-answers-None is operationally identical to verb-is-not-pytest and the derivation is unpinned. What HELD: US0480 is a real ratchet, refusing by identity against the live 371-entry baseline with no line-number brittleness; all 16 Verify nodeids resolve and pass; lane-check clean. Filed as BG0521, BG0523, BG0524 and carried under D0129. |

| BG0527 | qa+engineering | sdlc-studio | 2026-08-06 | Two independent seats at the batch boundary, each briefed by critic.py brief with per-unit fingerprints, bounded to each unit's declared Affects against base 367459cd. 65 mutants between them, 8 survivors triaged: 3 confirmed equivalent by construction, 5 real. Five of seven units REJECTed. Every wiring probe driven through the shipped CLI in throwaway fixtures. Blocking findings repaired at ba543ee2 with one exception carried unmet and stated: US0632 AC3, whose narrowing the seats proved false - the engine counts regex occurrences rather than AST nodes, and only the enumerator excludes multiline-string spans, so a mutant enumerated at one line is applied at another. That desync is filed as BG0533 and the criterion is carried rather than redefined. Both seats independently confirmed the two mutants the author called equivalent, and ruled the 60% overlap ceiling principled after killing it from both sides. |

| BG0541 | qa-seat-closing-review | sdlc-studio-authoring-session | 2026-08-07 | Adversarial delivery review over the run's whole batch, bounded per unit to its declared Affects against base ref 437713f7. | BG0558 | qa + engineering + product seats, independent subagents over four rounds | Claude Opus 5 session 6ee2f0cb | 2026-08-10 | Four adversarial rounds. Round 1 rejected all five original plans before a line existed. Round 2 rejected the delivery on six blocking findings including 41 files of mutation residue committed to main, a --boundary regression, and two declared mutants that survived their own criteria. | BG0457 | adversarial review seat (independent pass over the BG0457 repair) | sdlc-studio-authoring-session | 2026-08-15 | [pre-existing] The pass ran and APPROVED the repair: every mutant the criteria named was killed and the diff is strictly stronger than what it replaced. It then found the next layer down and that finding was FILED as BG0571 - three spec-agreement guards checking that words co-occur rather than that the rule holds, each verified by executing the mutant. Two of the three were INVERTED rather than weak: the opposite statement satisfied them. None was a regression - each survived against the guards as they stood before the repair - so the repair did not introduce them and did not hold the gate. BG0571 is now Fixed. This entry records evidence that was produced and never written to the ledger, not a pass run today. |

| US0671 | engineering, product and qa seats (subagents, delivery round 2) | authoring session | 2026-08-24 | Delivery review round 2: three seats - engineering, product and qa - each briefed with `critic.py brief` per unit and each running in its own git worktree, over all six units against the run's base ref da4beda1. Eighteen verdicts recorded in critic-verdicts.md. Four units were REJECTed on five blocking findings, every one established by EXECUTION rather than by reading: the derived-depth seal judging only the first span in a field, proved by position-dependence; the fixture counts still not pairwise distinct after round 1's repair, proved by three swap mutants surviving the criterion's own verifier beside a live positive control; the boundary lane's own call to the yield recorder unpinned, proved by replacing the call site with `pass` and watching all ten lane tests pass; the exemption path pattern blind to non-source production files, proved end-to-end on a fixture and measured live on BG0560 AC1; and a release-notes disclosure paragraph this batch's own commit made false, proved against `known\_issues.py --bar`. All five were repaired at the close and each repair is pinned by a mutant that was applied and killed. A separate independent test-plan plan review ran over all six units and REJECTed three of them on six plan rows whose declared mutant cannot fail the test their criterion names - filed as BG0606 and carried, with CR0554 to make that comparison mechanical. Round 1 had covered only the first wave, so US0674 and US0676 reached an independent reviewer for the first time in this round. |
